§ 10.01 TITLE.
   This codification of the general ordinances of the County of Boundary is
declared to be the official code of ordinances of the County of Boundary.
§ 10.02 ACCEPTANCE.
   Any official copy of the code shall be received without further proof in all
courts and in all administrative tribunals of the state.
§ 10.03 AMENDMENTS.
   (A)   Any ordinance amending the code shall set forth the title, chapter,
and section number of the section or sections to be amended. All such
ordinances shall comply with the provisions of the code and Idaho Code § 50-9,
regarding passage, execution, and publication of ordinances.
   (B)   All such amendments shall be promptly forwarded to the codifiers by
the County Clerk, and such ordinance shall be promptly published and inserted
in its proper place in each copy of the county code.
§ 10.04 MAINTENANCE OF COUNTY CODE.
   (A)   Upon receipt of each published ordinance from the codifiers, the
County Clerk shall promptly forward a copy of each page thereof to each person
having an official copy of the county code.
   (B)   The County Clerk may collect a fee for each copy of the county code
and an annual fee for maintenance thereof; provided, that such fees shall be
established by resolution or ordinance duly passed by the Board of County
Commissioners.
§ 10.05 REPEAL OF GENERAL ORDINANCES.
   All general ordinances of the county are hereby repealed, except as herein
specifically preserved or are, by implication, reserved from repeal, subject to
the saving clauses herein contained, excluding, without limitation, the special
ordinances relating to the following subjects: tax levies; appropriation of
public moneys; boundaries and annexations; franchises; ordinances granting
special rights to persons or correspondence; contracts; issuance of warrants;
public salaries; ordinances establishing name or vacating streets, alleys or
other public places; improvement districts; bonds; local elections; the sale or
exchange of real estate; sign ordinances; economic development; urban renewal;
public utilities; zoning; and all special ordinances of any kind, except for
those provisions in such special ordinances which the code expressly amends.
§ 10.06 PUBLIC UTILITY ORDINANCES.
   No ordinance or section thereof relating to railroad or railroad crossings
of streets and other public ways, or relating to the conduct, duties, service,
or rates of public utilities, shall be repealed by the adoption of the code or
by §
10.05, except as this code specifically provides for such repeal.
§ 10.07 SAVING CLAUSE.
   Any ordinance amending or repealing any section of the code shall not be
deemed or construed to abate any pending action based upon such amended or
repealed section, nor shall the passage of such ordinance prevent the arrest,
prosecution, and punishment of any violation of such section committed prior to
the effective date of such ordinance.
§ 10.08 GENERAL CONSTRUCTIONAL RULE.
   Except as expressly stated in the code, all words shall have their ordinary,
generally accepted meaning.
§ 10.09 PLURAL AND SINGULAR WORDS.
   Whenever any word in the code is used in either the singular or in the
plural form, then such word shall be deemed to include both the plural and
singular forms of such word, unless the context indicates an intent otherwise.
§ 10.10 MASCULINE AND FEMININE GENDER.
   When any person is referred to in any section of the code by use of the
masculine gender, then such reference shall be deemed to include the feminine
gender unless the context indicates an intent otherwise.
§ 10.11 PRIORITY OF ORDINANCE.
   In the event of any discrepancy or conflict between the code and any
ordinance passing or amending the same section of the code, then the provisions
of the ordinance shall prevail.
§ 10.12 DEFINITIONS.
   For the purpose of this code of ordinances, the following definitions shall
apply unless the context clearly indicates or requires a different meaning.
   AGENT. A person acting on behalf of another.
   BOARD. The Board of Commissioners of the County of Boundary, Idaho.
   COUNTY. The County of Boundary, Idaho.
   CRIME. An act in violation of this code.
   FELONY. A crime as defined under Idaho Code § 18-111.
   IDAPA. Idaho Administrative Regulations. A numbering designation for all
administrative rules in the state which denotes rules promulgated in accordance
with the State Administrative Procedure Act, being Idaho Code § 67-5201 through
67-5292.
   INFRACTION. A civil public offense, not constituting a crime, which is
punishable only by a penalty not exceeding $100 and for which no incarceration
may be imposed.
   LICENSE. The permission granted for the carrying on of a business, trade,
profession, or occupation.
   MISDEMEANOR. Every crime, except a felony.
   OCCUPANT. Any person who occupies the whole or any part of such building or
land, whether alone or with others.
   OFFENSE. A crime or other violation of this code.
   OPERATOR. The person who is generally in charge of or responsible for
conducting any business, profession or enterprise.
   OWNER. Any person owning real or personal property, including any part
owner, joint owner, tenant in common, joint tenant, remainderman, or person
holding a life estate or reversionary interest of any kind.
   PERSON. A human being and any public or private corporation, firm,
partnership, trust, estate, sole proprietorship, association, organization,
government, or any other entity recognized under state law.
   PERSONAL PROPERTY. Money, goods, chattels, effects, rights in action, and
all written instruments evidencing any pecuniary obligation.
   PROSECUTOR. The Prosecuting Attorney of Boundary County.
   RIGHT-OF-WAY. The privilege of the immediate use of the roadway or other
property.
   STATE. The State of Idaho.
   STREET. All public roads, ways, alleys, and rights-of-way used for the
movement of vehicular traffic, including any public sidewalks adjacent thereto.
   TENANT. Any person who occupies any building or real property for a
consideration to the owner.
§ 10.13 CAPTIONS.
   The captions and titles used at the commencement of each section or division
of the code are used only to indicate the content of the section and shall not
limit, modify or in any manner affect the scope, meaning, or intent of the
provisions thereafter.
§ 10.14 SEVERABILITY.
   The provisions of this code are hereby declared to be severable and if any
provision of this code or the application of such provision to any person or
circumstance is declared invalid for any reason, such declaration shall not
affect the validity of the remaining portions of this code.
§ 10.99 PENALTY.
   (A)   Misdemeanor penalty. Whenever any act or omission constitutes a crime
not constituting a felony or infraction and no specific penalty is stated in
the code, such crime shall be punishable by a fine not to exceed $300 or by
imprisonment for not more than six months, or by both such fine and
imprisonment for any single violation.
   (B)   Infractions. Whenever any person commits an infraction and no specific
penalty is provided for such act, such person shall be punished by a fine not
exceeding $100 and no imprisonment may be imposed.
   (C)   Applicability of penalty. The penalties provided in this section shall
be applied as though they were a part of every separate section of the code.
   (D)   Multiple violations. Whenever the same person violates the same
section of the code on two or more days, then each violation on each day shall
be deemed a separate offense and shall be separately punishable for each such
offense.
   (E)   Prosecutorial discretion. Whenever the same offense, act or omission
is punishable under different sections of the code, the county may elect under
which section to proceed; but no more than one sentence of imprisonment may be
imposed against the same person for the same offense; provided, that revocation
of a license or permit shall not be considered a penalty so as to bar
imposition of a penalty under the code.
   (F)   Penalty against officers. The penalty provisions of this section shall
not apply to any section of the code which designates the duties of any officer
or employee of the county unless the intention to impose a fine or penalty is
specifically and clearly expressed in the section creating the duty.

**** BOARD OF COUNTY COMMISSIONERS ****
§ 30.01 REGULAR MEETING DAYS.
   Unless otherwise a legal holiday, the Board of County Commissioners will
meet at the courthouse in regular session each Monday and Tuesday from 9:00
a.m. until 12:00 p.m. and from 1:30 p.m. until 5:00 p.m. or until all business
before them has been addressed beginning on January 14, 2013.
(Ord. 2013-01, passed 2-19-2013)
**** INDUSTRIAL DEVELOPMENT CORPORATION ****
§ 30.15 DEFINITIONS.
   For the purpose of this subchapter, the following definitions apply unless
the context clearly indicates or requires a different meaning.
   ACT. Idaho Code § 50-27.
   BOARD OF DIRECTORS. The Board of Directors of the public corporation.
   CORPORATION. The Industrial Development Corporation of the county.
   COUNTY. The County of Boundary, Idaho.
   FACILITIES. Land, rights in land, buildings, structures, docks, wharves,
machinery, transmission equipment, landscaping, utilities, approaches, roadways
and parking, handling and storage areas, and similar ancillary facilities.
   INDUSTRIAL DEVELOPMENT FACILITY or INDUSTRIAL DEVELOPMENT FACILITIES.
Manufacturing, processing, production, assembly, warehousing, solid waste
disposal, recreation and energy facilities, excluding facilities to transmit,
distribute, or produce electrical energy.
(Ord. 2004-03, passed 9-27-2004)
§ 30.16 INDUSTRIAL DEVELOPMENT CORPORATION.
   (A)   The county does hereby create a public corporation to carry out the
purpose of Idaho Code § 50-27 (the “Act”), as the law may from time to time
hereinafter be amended.
   (B)   The name of the public corporation shall be “The Industrial
Development Corporation of Boundary County, Idaho” (the “Corporation”).
   (C)   The Corporation shall have all the powers and be subject to all of the
limitations and provisions contained in the Act, as the law may hereinafter
from time to time be amended.
(Ord. 2004-03, passed 9-27-2004)
§ 30.17 CHARTER.
   A charter shall be issued to the Corporation by the Board of County
Commissioners, which shall grant to the Corporation authority to act pursuant
to the Act. A copy of the initial charter to be issued is attached to this
codified ordinance marked Exhibit “A.” Said charter may be amended from time to
time by the Board of County Commissioners, as constituted from time to time. A
majority of the members of the Board of Directors shall constitute a quorum.
(Ord. 2004-03, passed 9-27-2004)
§ 30.18 BOARD OF DIRECTORS.
   (A)   The Board of Directors of the Corporation shall be composed of those
members of the Board of County Commissioners as constituted from time to time
and those members appointed by the Board of County Commissioners. A majority of
the members of the Board of Directors shall constitute a quorum.
   (B)   (1)   The affairs of the Corporation shall be conducted and carried
out by the Board of Directors. The Board shall elect among its own members a
President, a Vice President, a Treasurer, and a Secretary, one of whom shall be
eligible to hold two offices. The Board of Directors shall adopt bylaws (rules
and regulations) governing the conduct of the Corporation.
      (2)   Members of the Board of Directors shall serve with such
compensation as may be provided in the bylaws to be adopted by the Board of
Directors.
(Ord. 2004-03, passed 9-27-2004)
§ 30.19 FINANCIAL INTEREST PROHIBITED.
   No director, officer, agent, or employee of the Corporation shall have
directly or indirectly any financial interest in any property to be included in
or any contract for property, service, or materials to be furnished or used in
connection with any industrial development facilities financed through the
Corporation.
(Ord. 2004-03, passed 9-27-2004)
§ 30.20 POWERS.
   The Corporation shall have all the powers granted by, as well as limited by,
the Act and shall include the following:
   (A)   To construct and maintain one or more industrial development
facilities;
   (B)   To lease to a lessee all or any part of any Industrial Development
Facility for such rentals and upon such terms and condition, including options
to purchase, as its Board of Directors considers advisable and not in conflict
with the Act;
   (C)   To sell by installment contract or otherwise and convey all or any
part of any industrial development facility for such purchase price and upon
such terms and conditions as its Board of Directors considers advisable, which
are not in conflict with the Act;
   (D)   To make loans for the purpose of providing temporary or permanent
financing or refinancing of all or part of the project costs for any industrial
development facility, including the refunding of any outstanding obligations,
mortgages, or advances issued, made, or given by any person for the project
costs; and to charge and collect interest on the loans for the loan payments
upon such terms and conditions as its Board of Directors considers advisable
which are not in conflict with the Act;
   (E)   To issue revenue bonds for the purpose of financing all or part of the
project cost of any industrial development facility and to secure the payment
of the revenue bonds as provided in the Act;
   (F)   Add security for the payment of the principal and interest on any
revenue bonds issued for any agreements made in connection therewith, to
mortgage, pledge, or otherwise encumber any or all of its industrial
development facilities or any part or parts thereof, whether then owned or
thereafter acquired, and to assign any lease or mortgage and repledge any
security conveyed to the Corporation, to secure any loan made by the
Corporation, and to pledge the revenues and receipts therefrom;
   (G)   To sue and be sued, complain, and defend in its corporate name;
   (H)   To make contracts and to execute all instruments necessary or
convenient for the carrying out of its business;
   (I)   To have a corporate seal and to use the same by causing it, or a
facsimile thereof, to be impressed or affixed or in any other manner
reproduced;
   (J)   Subject to the limitations of Idaho Code § 50-2706, to borrow money,
accept grants from, or contracts with any local, state, or federal governmental
agency or with any financial, public, or private corporation;
   (K)   To make and alter bylaws not inconsistent with its charter for the
administration and regulation of the affairs of the Corporation;
   (L)   To collect fees or charges from users or prospective users of
industrial development facilities to recover actual or anticipated
administrative costs;
   (M)   To execute financing documents incidental to the powers enumerated in
this section;
   (N)   The Corporation created under this subchapter may not operate any
industrial development facilities as a business other than as lessor, seller,
or lender. The purpose and holding of mortgages, deeds of trust, or other
security interests and contracting from any servicing thereof is not considered
the operation of any industrial development facility;
   (O)   The Corporation created by this subchapter may not exercise any of the
powers authorized in this section or issue any revenue bonds with respect to
any industrial development facility unless the industrial development facility
is located wholly within the boundaries of the county;
   (P)   The county may not give or lend any money or property nor exercise its
power of eminent domain in aid of the Corporation. This provision shall not
preclude the county from dealing with the Corporation on an arms-length basis;
and
   (Q)   The Corporation may not issue revenue obligations except upon approval
of the county, and upon approval of the city within whose planning jurisdiction
the proposed industrial development facility lies.
(Ord. 2004-03, passed 9-27-2004)
§ 30.21 LIABILITY.
   Every person who was or is a party, or is threatened to be made a party to,
or is involved in any action, suit, or proceeding, whether civil, criminal,
administrative, or investigative, by reason of the fact that he or she or a
person of whom he or she is the legal representative is or was a director or
officer of the Corporation, or officer of another corporation, or as its
representative in a partnership joint venture, trust, or other enterprise,
shall be indemnified and held harmless to the fullest extent legally
permissible under the law of the state from time to time against all expense,
liability, and loss (including attorneys’ fees, judgments, fines, and amounts
paid or to be paid in settlement), reasonably incurred or suffered by him or
her in connection therewith. Such rights of indemnification shall be a contract
right which may be enforced in any manner desired by such person. Such right of
indemnification shall not be exclusive of any other right which such directors,
officers, or representatives may have or thereafter acquire, and without
limiting the generality of such statement, they shall be entitled to their
respective rights of indemnification under any bylaw, agreement, vote of
stockholders, provision of law, or otherwise, as well as their rights under
this section. The Board of Directors may adopt bylaws from time to time with
respect to indemnification to provide at all times the fullest indemnification
permitted by laws of the state, and may cause the Corporation to purchase and
maintain insurance on behalf of any person who is or was a director or officer
of the Corporation as a director or officer of another corporation, or as its
representative in a partnership, joint venture, trust, or other enterprise,
against any liability asserted against such person and incurred in any such
capacity or arising out of such status, whether or not the Corporation would
have the power to indemnify such person.
(Ord. 2004-03, passed 9-27-2004)
§ 30.22 NET EARNINGS.
   Any net earnings of the Corporation, beyond those necessary for retirement
of indebtedness incurred by it, shall inure to the county and not for the
benefit of any other person. Alteration of the charter of or dissolution of or
audits of the Corporation shall be provided by the Act and by subsequent
ordinances of the county. Upon dissolution of the Corporation, title to all
property owned by the Corporation shall vest in the county.
(Ord. 2004-03, passed 9-27-2004)

§ 31.01 CREATION OF RIGHT.
   The people of the county shall have the right to enact ordinances through
the initiative process, and to repeal ordinances through the referendum
process, according to the procedures set forth in this chapter.
(Ord. 80, passed 3-3-1980)
§ 31.02 NUMBER OF PETITIONERS REQUIRED.
   To enact an ordinance by initiative or to repeal an ordinance by referendum,
there shall be attached to the petition the signatures of legal voters of the
county equal to at least 20% of the total number of voters registered to vote
at the last general election in the county.
(Ord. 80, passed 3-3-1980)
§ 31.03 FORM OF PETITION.
   (A)   The initiative petition shall be in substantially the following form.
 
Initiative Petition to the Board of Boundary County Commissioners of Boundary
County, Idaho: “We, the undersigned citizens and legal voters of Boundary
County, respectfully demand that the following proposed ordinance, to-wit: (set
out full text of measure proposed) shall be submitted to the legal voters at an
election to be called in accordance with Idaho Code § 34-18, and each for
himself or herself says: I have personally signed this petition; I am a legal
voter of Boundary County; my residence and post office are correctly written
after my name.”
Name_________________ Street and Number ___________________ City and Post
Office ___________________________________
(Here follow numbered lines for signature)
 
   (B)   The petition for referendum on any ordinance passed by the Board of
County Commissioners shall be in substantially the same form with appropriate
title and changes, setting out in full the text of the ordinance to be referred
to the people for their approval or rejection.
(Ord. 80, passed 3-3-1980)
§ 31.04 TIME FOR FILING PETITIONS.
   Referendum petitions with the requisite number of signatures attached shall
be filed with the County Clerk not less than 60 days following the final
adoption of the ordinance to be subject to referendum.
(Ord. 80, passed 3-3-1980)
§ 31.05 PRINTING OF PETITION AND SHEETS FOR SIGNATURES; TIME LIMITS FOR
PERFECTING PETITION.
   (A)   Before or at the time of beginning to circulate any petition for
initiative or referendum, the person or persons, organization or organizations
under whose authority the petition is to be circulated, shall send or deliver
to the County Clerk a copy of the petition duly signed by at least 20 electors
eligible to sign such petition. The County Clerk shall immediately examine the
petition and specify the form and kind and size of paper on which the petition
shall be printed and circulated for further signatures. All petitions and
sheets for signatures shall be printed on a good quality bond or ledger paper,
on pages eight and one-half inches in width by 13 inches in length, with a
margin of one and three-fourths inch at the top for binding, and the sheets for
signatures shall have numbered lines thereon from one to 20 for signatures. The
petition shall be prepared in sections, with each section numbered
consecutively. Each section of a petition must have a printed copy of the
petition as the first page, and each section shall have attached to it no more
than ten sheets for signatures.
   (B)   The County Clerk shall indicate in writing on the petition that it is
approved as to form, the County Clerk shall inform the person or persons,
organization or organizations under whose authority the petition is to be
circulated, in writing, that the petition must be perfected with the required
number of certified signatures within 60 days following the date of approval as
to form. Any petition not perfected with the required number of certified
signatures within the 60 days allowed shall be declared null and void ab initio
in its entirety.
(Ord. 80, passed 3-3-1980)
§ 31.06 VERIFICATION ON SHEETS FOR SIGNATURES.
   Each signature sheet of each petition containing signatures shall be
verified in substantially the following form by the person who circulated said
sheet of the petition, by his or her affidavit hereon, as a part thereof.
 
STATE OF IDAHO:
               ss.
County of Boundary:
I, ______________________, swear, under penalty of perjury, that every person who signed this sheet of the
foregoing petition signed his or her name thereto in my presence. I believe that each has stated his or her
name and the accompanying required information on the signature sheet correctly, and that the person was
eligible to sign this petition.
Signature ________________________
Post Office Address __________________________
Subscribed and sworn to before me this _________ day of ____________________, 20__.
                                 Notary Public ____________________________
(Notary Seal)      Residing at _______________________________
 
(Ord. 80, passed 3-3-1980)
§ 31.07 EXAMINATION AND CERTIFICATION OF SIGNATURES.
   (A)   All petitions with attached signature sheets shall be presented on the
same day to the County Clerk, who shall make a cursory examination of them to
determine whether the petitions apparently contain the necessary number of
signatures.
      (1)   If the total number of signatures on the petition is not sufficient
to satisfy the number required by §
31.02, all petitions with attached signature sheets shall be retained by the
County Clerk who shall notify in writing the person filing the petition of the
number of signatures needed, and further signatures may be gathered, if within
the time limit of §
31.05.
      (2)   If the cursory examination of the signature sheets reveals:
         (a)   Erasures on any signature;
         (b)   Illegible or undecipherable signatures;
         (c)   Signatures not properly identified by all of the information
required on the sheet;
         (d)   Duplicate signatures; or
         (e)   Signatures of persons who have requested in writing to have his,
her, or their names removed from the petition, the County Clerk shall summarily
reject such signatures and they shall not be counted.
      (3)   Each rejected signature shall be drawn through with ink and
initialed by the County Clerk. If the total number of signatures not rejected
is not sufficient to satisfy the number required by §
31.02, all petitions with attached signature sheets shall be retained by the
County Clerk who shall notify in writing the person filing the petition of the
number of signatures needed, and further signatures may be gathered, if within
the time limit of §
31.05.
   (B)   All petitions presented to the County Clerk found to apparently
contain the necessary number of signatures, after the cursory examination
provided for above, shall be filed with the County Clerk and become public
records of the county not to be returned. The County Clerk shall examine each
such signature purported to be that of a registered elector of the county, and
compare each such signature with the registration documents available to the
County Clerk. The County Clerk shall summarily reject and not count all
signatures which are not the signatures of a registered elector of the county.
Rejected signatures shall be drawn through with ink and initialed by the County
Clerk. The County Clerk may take up to, but not more than, ten days after
filing of the petition to complete this examination. The County Clerk shall
certify each signature found to comply with all of the requirements of this
chapter by an appropriate mark following each signature.
   (C)   The County Clerk shall total the number of certified signatures, and
if found to total the number of signatures required by §
31.02, shall proceed as provided in §
31.05.
(Ord. 80, passed 3-3-1980)
§ 31.08 SUFFICIENCY OF PETITION; NOTIFICATION; EFFECT OF THE BOARD OF COUNTY
COMMISSIONERS; ACTION; ELECTION.
   (A)   In the event a petition filed with the County Clerk does not contain
the required number of certified signatures, the County Clerk shall inform the
person or organization under whose authority the petition was circulated that
the petition is defective for lack of certified signatures, and specify the
number of additional signatures required to make the petition valid. The
petition must be perfected within 30 days of the date the County Clerk finds
the petition defective for lack of certified signatures. If the petition is not
perfected within the 30-day period, the County Clerk shall declare the petition
null and void ab initio in its entirety.
   (B)   In the event a petition filed with the County Clerk is found by the
County Clerk to contain the required number of certified signatures, the County
Clerk shall promptly, by certified mail, inform the petitioners and shall also
notify the Board of County Commissioners at its next meeting, that the
initiative or referendum petition is in proper form.
      (1)   If the petition is for a referendum, the Board of County
Commissioners shall have 30 days from the date of certification of the petition
to repeal the ordinance being referred to the voters. In the event the Board of
County Commissioners repeal the ordinance, the referendum petition shall be
declared null and void.
      (2)   If the petition is an initiative petition, the Board of County
Commissioners shall have 30 days to pass an ordinance substantially as proposed
by the petition. In the event the Board of County Commissioners pass such an
ordinance, the initiative petition shall be null and void.
      (3)   In the event the Board of County Commissioners neither repeal the
ordinance which is the subject of a referendum petition, nor enact an ordinance
which is the subject of an initiative petition, an election shall be ordered by
the County Clerk to be conducted county-wide. A special election for initiative
or referendum shall be provided not more than 90 days following the
certification of the petition, provided that in the event a county election
will occur within the 90 days, the initiative or referendum shall be submitted
at the time of the county election.
(Ord. 80, passed 3-3-1980)
§ 31.09 FORM OF BALLOT.
   The Board of County Commissioners shall prepare a ballot for an initiative
or referendum election in one of the following ways:
   (A)   If the full text of the ordinance or proposed ordinance to be voted
upon does not exceed 100 words in length, it may be set out in full on the
election ballot; or
   (B)   If the full text of the ordinance or proposed ordinance to be voted
upon exceeds 100 words in length, and the Board of County Commissioners vote
not to have it printed at length on the election ballot, they shall prepare a
short title and description of the ordinance or proposed ordinance which shall
clearly and impartially state its purpose and effect, which short title and
description shall be printed on the election ballot.
(Ord. 80, passed 3-3-1980)
§ 31.10 CONDUCT OF ELECTION.
   Initiative or referendum elections, whether special or general, shall be
conducted and the results thereof canvassed and certified in all respects as
near as practicable, in like manner as general elections, except as otherwise
provided.
(Ord. 80, passed 3-3-1980)
§ 31.11 CANVASS OF RETURNS.
   The Board of County Commissioners shall meet within five days after said
election to canvass the votes and the County Clerk shall immediately announce
the results. Any initiative measure approved by a majority of the votes cast
shall be in full force and effect from the date of such announcement. Any
referendum measure for which a majority of the votes were cast in favor of
repealing an ordinance shall be effective on the date of such announcement.
(Ord. 80, passed 3-3-1980)
§ 31.12 PROHIBITED ACTS.
   The following acts are prohibited:
   (A)   Signs any name other than his or her own to any initiative or
referendum petition;
   (B)   Knowingly signs his or her name to any initiative or referendum
petition if he or she is not a registered county elector;
   (C)   Willfully or knowingly circulates, publishes, or exhibits any false
statement or representation concerning the contents, purport, or effect of any
initiative or referendum petition for the purpose of obtaining any signature to
any such petition, or for the purpose of persuading any person to sign any such
petition;
   (D)   Circulates or causes to circulate any initiative or referendum
petition, knowing the same to contain false, forged, or fictitious names; and/
or
   (E)   Makes any false affidavit concerning any initiative or referendum
petition or the signatures appended thereto.
(Ord. 80, passed 3-3-1980) Penalty, see §
31.99
§ 31.13 LIMITATION.
   Nothing within the terms and language of this chapter should be considered
or interpreted to allow for procedures herein to divest or otherwise interfere
with the normal powers and duties of the Board of County Commissioners with
regards to its right to adopt or repeal any ordinance pursuant to state law.
Nothing in the language of this chapter should be considered or interpreted to
allow for the violation of substantive or procedure requirements of state law.
(Ord. 80, passed 3-3-1980)
§ 31.99 PENALTY.
   A person is guilty of a misdemeanor, and shall be subject to punishment of
30 days in the county jail and/or a fine of $300, who commits and is found
guilty of the acts listed in §
31.12.
(Ord. 80, passed 3-3-1980)

§ 32.01 ESTABLISHMENT OF A SERVICE AREA.
   The consolidated emergency communications system for the county shall have a
service area comprising the whole of the county including incorporated
municipalities.
(Ord. 2009-1, passed 12-22-2008)
§ 32.02 FEES.
   Pursuant to the authority granted to this Board pursuant to Idaho Code § 31-
4819, the Board of County Commissioners hereby enacts the emergency
communications fee in an amount of $1 per month per access or interconnected
VoIP service line.
(Ord. 2009-1, passed 12-22-2008)
§ 32.03 ESTABLISHMENT OF EMERGENCY COMMUNICATIONS GOVERNING BOARD (ECB).
   There is hereby established an Emergency Communications Governing Board
(ECB) for the county. The membership of the ECB shall be composed of the Board
of County Commissioners.
(Ord. 2009-1, passed 12-22-2008)
§ 32.04 COMPOSITION OF THE ECB.
   The Board of County Commissioners may by resolution amend the composition of
the ECB, as well as appoint an Advisory Board to assist the ECB.
(Ord. 2009-1, passed 12-22-2008)
§ 32.05 ADMINISTRATOR OF SYSTEM.
   The Board of County Commissioners is hereby appointed as the Administrator
of the emergency communications system.
(Ord. 2009-1, passed 12-22-2008)
§ 32.06 SERVICE OF SYSTEM.
   The Sheriff’s office shall be designated to service the 9-1-1 calls. The
Board of County Commissioners may by resolution designate a different provider
of service for the system.
(Ord. 2009-1, passed 12-22-2008)

**** GENERAL PROVISIONS ****
§ 50.01 PURPOSE.
   It is the purpose of this subchapter to institute within the county an
orderly solid waste collection system and to provide for protection of public
health, environmental quality, public safety, and welfare.
(Ord. 98-1, passed 10-14-1997)
§ 50.02 DEFINITIONS.
   For the purpose of this subchapter, the following definitions apply unless
the context clearly indicates or requires a different meaning.
   COMMERCIAL SOLID WASTE HAULER. Any person who collects, hauls, or transports
solid waste on any highway or county road for business or profit or
remuneration.
   DESIGNATED DISPOSAL SITES. Wood, metal, household demolition, tires,
batteries, oil, antifreeze, and such other sites as may hereafter be adopted by
resolution of the Board of County Commissioners.
   DISPOSAL. The act or process of final placement, distribution, or
arrangement in an orderly manner of solid waste so as to deal with it
conclusively in compliance with Idaho Code and the rules and regulations
relating to solid waste.
   MANDATORY SOLID WASTE COLLECTION SYSTEM. A command requirement of assigning
the entire gathering or amassing of solid waste to a delegated agent to ensure
complete transport of the same to a disposal site.
   SOLID WASTE. All solid material that is considered to be useless, unwanted,
or discarded by any person in possession of it. This includes, but is not
restricted to, garbage, trash, rubbish, and construction waste.
   SYSTEM. Lands, sites, facilities, equipment, and manpower necessary for
collection, transportation, storage, treatment, processing, reuse, recycling,
or other means necessary for the disposal of solid waste.
(Ord. 98-1, passed 10-14-1997)
§ 50.03 ADMINISTRATION.
   (A)   Responsible agency. The county.
   (B)   Function and powers.
      (1)   As defined under Idaho Code §§ 31-4401 et seq.; and
      (2)   To establish an orderly solid waste collection and disposal system
and to establish the necessary policies to adequately administer said system.
(Ord. 98-1, passed 10-14-1997)
§ 50.04 SCOPE.
   (A)   Any person or commercial waste hauler who hauls solid waste to a
collection or disposal site must prevent littering, spilling, leaking, or
dripping upon county roads.
   (B)   All persons hauling solid waste to a collection or disposal site must
follow all rules and regulations of landfill by depositing all materials to
appropriate designated areas either designated by attendant or by sign.
   (C)   Any person who hauls solid waste to a collection or disposal site in
the form of wood products shall deposit said wood products in a “wood only”
pile, and no other debris shall be deposited in the “wood only” pile.
   (D)   Any person who hauls solid waste to a collection or disposal site in
the form of metal shall dispose of said metal in a pile designated “metal only”
by the attendant or by sign, and no other debris shall be placed in the “metal
only” pile.
   (E)   Any person who hauls solid waste to a collection or disposal site in
the form of tires shall dispose of said tires in a pile designated “tires only”
by the attendant or by sign, and no other debris shall be placed in the “tires
only” pile.
   (F)   Any person who hauls solid waste to a collection or disposal site must
obey all stop signs and a ten mph speed sign within the landfill.
(Ord. 98-1, passed 10-14-1997) Penalty, see §
50.99
§ 50.05 LIABILITY.
   The County Prosecutor, upon probable cause, shall cause proceedings to be
commenced against violators of the provisions of §
50.04 by civil or criminal action.
(Ord. 98-1, passed 10-14-1997)
**** COMMERCIAL WOOD/LAWN DISPOSAL PLAN ****
§ 50.20 POLICY.
   The County Solid Waste Department will accept only wood/lawn waste generated
within the county. Wood/Lawn waste that is delivered by anyone other than the
homeowner/tenant is delivered in a commercial vehicle, or is generated by a
commercial activity will be considered commercial waste with appropriate
charges applied. Patrons may be required to produce verification of county
residence or property ownership which indicates the current payment of the
solid waste disposal fee and verification that the waste was generated from
that residence. Documentation must be deemed suitable by solid waste personnel.
Failure to provide appropriate documentation may result in immediate charge of
the commercial rate.
(Ord. 2015-3, passed 9-21-2015)
§ 50.21 DEFINITIONS.
   For the purpose of this subchapter, the following definitions apply unless
the context clearly indicates or requires a different meaning.
   COMMERCIAL ACTIVITY. Waste resulting from activity, or service for profit
will be deemed a COMMERCIAL ACTIVITY, and disposal resulting from that activity
will be allowed at the current commercial rate.
   COMMERCIAL VEHICLE.
      (1)   Any vehicle that is used for commercial purposes, regardless of
license plate designation.
      (2)   Any vehicle displaying a business advertisement, or logo, including
rental trucks, vans, cars, or trailers.
   HOMEOWNER. The registered owner or household tenant of the residential
property for which a current solid waste fee has been paid.
(Ord. 2015-3, passed 9-21-2015)
§ 50.22 COMMERCIAL DISPOSAL CHARGE.
 
Brush/Lawn waste                        $6 per cubic yard
Clean stumps over six inches (at face)  $20 per cubic yard
Clean stumps under six inches (at face) $10 per cubic yard
Separated lumber                        $6 per cubic yard
Wood chips                              $6 per cubic yard
 
(Ord. 2015-3, passed 9-21-2015)
§ 50.99 PENALTY.
   (A)   Any person violating any provision of this chapter for which no
specific penalty is prescribed shall be subject to §
10.99 of this code of ordinances.
   (B)   (1)   Any person in violation of §
50.04 shall be guilty of a misdemeanor, and subject to a fine not more than
$300 and may be imprisoned for a term not to exceed six months.
      (2)   The county may also bring a civil action to abate or enjoin a
violation of §
50.04 and to collect and enforce payment of any sums of money due to the county
by reason of §§
50.01 through
50.05 together with reasonable legal fees and court costs.
(Ord. 98-1, passed 10-14-1997)
TITLE VII: TRAFFIC CODE
   Chapter
      
70.   TRAFFIC RULES AND REGULATIONS
CHAPTER 70: TRAFFIC RULES AND REGULATIONS
Section
   
70.01   Rights-of-way
   
70.02   Speed limit for county roads
§ 70.01 RIGHTS-OF-WAY.
   (A)   Purpose. The vast majority of land in the county is managed by a
federal or state agency. Ever since initial settlement of county lands, owners
of private land and of private property interests have, of necessity, asserted
and used rights-of-way upon and across such federally managed and state-managed
lands in order to obtain that beneficial and economically efficient use of his,
her, or their private land and property interests which is vital to the
commerce and economic stability of the county. The purpose of this section is
to promote and foster commerce and economic stability within the county by
authorizing the County Recorder to receive and record assertions of such
rights-of-way upon and across federally managed and state-managed lands.
   (B)   Recording of assertions of rights-of-way. The County Recorder shall
receive for recording and shall record assertions of rights-of-way which are
filed with the County Recorder on a form to be provided by the County Recorder.
   (C)   Form for filing assertion of rights-of-way. Any assertion of rights-
of-way must be filed on the form provided by the County Recorder and all
information required by said form shall be provided by the person asserting the
rights-of-way. The rights-of-way form must be signed by the person asserting
the rights-of-way and the signature must be notarized. The assertion of rights-
of-way must be accompanied by a legal description of the asserted rights-of-way
or a map which clearly designates the location of the asserted rights-of-way.
The County Recorder shall receive and record the rights-of-way form, the
supporting legal description, and/or map and other documents upon which the
assertion is based.
   (D)   Fee for recording. The County Recorder shall charge a recording fee in
accordance with state law.
(Ord. 98-2, passed 12-16-1997)
§ 70.02 SPEED LIMIT FOR COUNTY ROADS.
   The maximum speed on all county roads, unless said county roads are
otherwise posted, is 35 mph.
(Ord. 73-2, passed 4-9-1973)
