**** GENERAL PROVISIONS ****
§ 90.01 PURPOSE; TITLE.
   (A)   The purpose of this chapter is to provide standards and procedures for
responsible care and ownership of dogs and other domestic animals within the
unincorporated areas of the county. This chapter does not address all aspects
of animal ownership, rather, it establishes some minimum standards for animal
care, control, and treatment in the interest of public safety and animal
welfare. The responsibility for actions and behavior of domestic animals
remains with the owner.
   (B)   This chapter shall be referred as the “Boundary County Animal Control
Ordinance.”
(Ord. 2006-06, passed 8-7-2006)
§ 90.02 DEFINITIONS.
   For the purpose of this chapter, the following definitions apply unless the
context clearly indicates or requires a different meaning.
   ANIMAL. Shall include, but not be limited to, birds, fish, reptiles,
mammals, and nonhuman primates.
   ATTACK. Biting, attempted biting, acting in a threatening manner,
aggressively pursuing with a present ability to bite or injure a person. A
“threatening manner” is defined as any action by the animal towards a person
that instills a well-founded apprehension of fear in the person because of the
animal’s actions coupled with the present ability of the animal to do harm.
   AT LARGE. Being on any private property, without the permission of the
person who owns or has a right to possess or use the property, or unrestrained
by a leash on either public property or private property, or in any other place
or manner which presents substantial risk or interference with animal or public
health, safety, or welfare.
   BOARD OF COMMISSIONERS. The Boundary County Board of Commissioners.
   DOG. Any Canis familiaris of either sex.
   LEASH. Any rope, leather strap, chain, or any other material, being held in
the hand of a person capable of controlling, and actually controlling, the
animal to which it is attached.
   OWNER. Any person who is the legal owner, or any person who keeps, harbors,
takes care of for another person, or possesses or controls in any manner any
animal on such premises shall be considered the OWNER of said animal.
   RABIES CERTIFICATE FORM. A Rabies Certificate form issued by a licensed
veterinarian.
   SHERIFF. The County Sheriff, his or her agents, deputies, or other persons
designated by the Sheriff.
(Ord. 2006-06, passed 8-7-2006)
**** VACCINATIONS ****
§ 90.15 VACCINATION REQUIRED.
   Dog owners shall obtain a rabies vaccination for each dog they own, keep,
harbor, or possess within 30 days after it becomes six months of age, or within
30 days of obtaining any dog over six months of age. It shall be unlawful for
any person to keep, harbor, or possess any dog, as required, above unless such
dog has a current rabies vaccination, administered by a licensed veterinarian,
with a rabies vaccine approved for use in a dog. Such vaccination shall be
repeated at such intervals as determined by the type of vaccine used and
licensing requirements of said vaccine.
(Ord. 2006-06, passed 8-7-2006) Penalty, see §
90.99
§ 90.16 VACCINATION CERTIFICATE.
   Any veterinarian who vaccinates a dog for rabies shall also certify by
properly completing a Rabies Vaccine Certificate.
(Ord. 2006-06, passed 8-7-2006)
§ 90.17 REPORTING SUSPECTED CASES OF RABIES.
   Any person having care and custody of an animal which shows symptoms of
rabies or which acts in any manner that would lead to a reasonable suspicion
that the animal may have rabies shall notify the Sheriff and the Panhandle
Health District.
(Ord. 2006-06, passed 8-7-2006)
§ 90.18 REPORTING OF BITES.
   (A)   All persons bitten and parents or guardians of children bitten, by a
dog, cat, skunk, fox, coyote, bobcat, or other animal of a species subject to
rabies, shall notify the Sheriff and the Panhandle Health District as soon as
possible thereafter.
   (B)   Physicians treating such bites and other persons having knowledge of
such bites shall also notify the Sheriff and the Panhandle Health District.
(Ord. 2006-06, passed 8-7-2006)
§ 90.19 ISOLATION OF SUSPECTED RABID ANIMALS.
   (A)   Upon the order of the Sheriff or the Panhandle Health District, a
suspected rabid animal shall be isolated in strict confinement under the proper
care and observation of a licensed veterinarian, in a veterinary hospital,
animal shelter, or other facility, approved by the Sheriff or the Panhandle
Health District.
   (B)   Such animal shall not be killed or released for at least ten days
after the onset of symptoms, unless permission is obtained from the owner or
responsible health agency to kill the animal for purposes of laboratory
examination. All costs incurred associated with this chapter shall be paid by
the animal’s owner.
(Ord. 2006-06, passed 8-7-2006)
§ 90.20 TAG AFFIXED.
   Every dog owner shall securely affix the current rabies tag to the collar or
harness of the dog for which it was issued, and shall ensure that the dog wears
such rabies tag at all times.
(Ord. 2006-06, passed 8-7-2006)
§ 90.21 REMOVAL OF TAG.
   No person shall remove a rabies tag or identification tag from any dog which
is not owned by that person unless the person removing said tag is a licensed
veterinarian.
(Ord. 2006-06, passed 8-7-2006)
**** CARE AND CONTROL ****
§ 90.35 RESTRAINT OF DOGS BY OWNERS.
   Dog owners shall at all times prevent their dogs from being at large, and
from being in violation of other sections of this chapter. Failure of an owner
or handler to comply with any provision of this chapter shall be a violation.
Dogs may be free from a leash while participating in field trials and obedience
trials, while actively engaged in herding livestock, while assisting a peace
officer engaged in law enforcement duties, or while hunting pursuant to state
fish and game regulations, or under the immediate control of the owner when the
animal is on property open to the public. Dogs shall not be allowed to run at
large in the vicinity of any farm, pasture, ranch, dwelling, house, or
cultivated fields of another, without a competent and responsible attendant or
owner, or fail to keep such dog securely confined within the limits of an
owner’s premises when not under the immediate care and control of a competent
and responsible attendant or owner. In addition to the forgoing, any animal
which is injurious to the health, or acts in a threatening manner to any
person, or is an obstruction to the free use of property so as to interfere
with the comfortable enjoyment of life or property by any person or any animal
who obstructs unlawfully the free passage or use of any navigable lake, river,
stream, channel, basin, or any public park, square, street, highway, road,
whether it be county or state, said dog’s owners shall be subject to the
penalties identified within this chapter.
(Ord. 2006-06, passed 8-7-2006) Penalty, see §
90.99
§ 90.36 DOGS RUNNING AT LARGE TO BE SEIZED.
   The Sheriff may seize all dogs running at large when not under the immediate
care and control of a competent and responsible attendant or owner and impound
the same. Wherein such dog remains unclaimed after actual notice to the owner,
if known, for a period of 14 days, it shall be the duty of the Sheriff to cause
such dog to be disposed of. All costs associated with the boarding and disposal
of said animal shall be borne by the owner.
(Ord. 2006-06, passed 8-7-2006)
§ 90.37 FEES TO RECLAIM DOGS.
   The owner or any other person on the owner’s behalf or absent of a showing
of ownership any other person seeking to reclaim a dog which has been seized
for running at large without being under the immediate care and control of a
competent and responsible attendant or owner shall pay a fee of $25. When the
same dog has been seized a second time, the owner or other person seeking to
reclaim the dog shall pay a fee of $50. When the same dog has been seized a
subsequent time, the owner or person seeking to reclaim the dog shall pay a fee
of $100. Such fees shall be in addition to any fine and/or jail term imposed,
if criminal proceedings are commenced in accordance with this chapter. Said fee
shall be paid to the Sheriff prior to retrieval of the dog.
(Ord. 2006-06, passed 8-7-2006)
§ 90.38 CRUEL TREATMENT AND ABANDONMENT.
   No person shall treat an animal in a cruel manner as defined in Idaho Code §
25-35. No person shall abandon any animal on any public or private property, or
allow an animal to be without potable water and food.
(Ord. 2006-06, passed 8-7-2006) Penalty, see §
90.99
§ 90.39 ANIMALS IN UNATTENDED VEHICLES.
   No person shall leave an animal in any unattended vehicle without adequate
ventilation or security to protect the public, or in any manner as to subject
the animal to extreme temperatures which adversely affect the health or welfare
of the animal.
(Ord. 2006-06, passed 8-7-2006) Penalty, see §
90.99
§ 90.40 AUTHORITY TO IMPOUND.
   Any peace officer, animal control officer, or any other person designated by
the Sheriff may seize and impound any animal running at large without a collar
or harness bearing evidence of current vaccination as required by this chapter
as well as any and all dogs or other animals found running at large upon the
public streets, alleys, or other public place, or private premises without the
permission of the owner of said premises.
(Ord. 2006-06, passed 8-7-2006)
§ 90.41 VICIOUS DOGS.
   (A)   (1)   Any dog which, when not physically provoked, attacks as defined,
wounds, bites, or otherwise injures any person that results in lacerations,
puncture wounds, muscle tears, or disfiguration, or requires any sutures or
corrective or cosmetic surgery to a person, or any dog not physically provoked
physically attacks, wounds, bites, threatens to bite or attack, or otherwise
injures any person or livestock, poultry, domesticated animals, or animals kept
in captivity for commercial purposes is VICIOUS. Working dogs, including, but
not limited to, guard dogs, police dogs, and stock dogs, are exempt from this
classification while performing their specified duties. Once a dog is deemed
VICIOUS by the court or by the Sheriff, it shall be unlawful for the owner of
the vicious dog to allow to be present outside a secured enclosure unless it is
restrained as required by this chapter.
      (2)   A SECURED ENCLOSURE is one from which the animal cannot escape and
for which exit and entry is controlled by the owner of the premises or the
owner of the animal. A SECURED ENCLOSURE shall at minimum either be confined
indoors in a manner where escape is not possible or outdoors in a locked pen or
enclosure designed to keep the animal from escaping and shall have secure sides
with a secure top and a concrete floor that is attached to the sides of the pen
or structure. All pens or structures must be kept clean and sanitary and must
protect the animals from the elements.
   (B)   (1)   Leash and muzzle. The owner of a vicious dog shall not allow the
dog to go outside its kennel, pen, or structure unless the dog is muzzled,
restrained by a chain or leash not more than four feet in length, and under the
physical control of an owner. The muzzle must not cause injury to the dog or
interfere with its vision or respiration, but must prevent the dog from biting
any human or animal.
      (2)   Signs. The owner of a vicious dog shall display in a prominent
place on the owner’s premises a clearly visible warning sign of two feet by two
feet in dimension indicating that there is a vicious dog on the premises. The
sign must be readable from the public highway or thoroughfare and posted at the
entrance of the owner’s property. The owner shall also display a sign with a
symbol warning children of the presence of a vicious dog. Similar signs shall
be posted on the dog’s kennel, pen, and enclosed structure.
      (3)   Insurance. The owner of a vicious dog must provide proof to the
County Clerk that the owner has procured public liability insurance of a least
$500,000 insuring the owner for any damage or personal injury which may be
caused by his or her vicious dog. If any time the insurance, as provided,
herein is not in effect, the dog may be picked up and placed in protective
custody at a place designated by the county. The dog owner is required to have
his or her insurance company notify the county if the insurance expires or
lapses.
      (4)   Inspection on premises. The owner of a vicious dog shall contact
the Sheriff’s Department on an annual basis and shall arrange for an inspection
of the premises wherein the vicious dog is confined. The inspection shall deal
with the security of the confinement and all other items listed above,
including the insurance. The fee for such inspection shall be not less than $50
per animal.
      (5)   Identification. The owner shall provide front and profile pictures
of any animal declared to be vicious, to be kept on file at the Sheriff’s
office.
   (C)   Persons guilty of a violation of any portion of this chapter, in
addition to any civil liability available and in accordance with law, shall be
guilty of a misdemeanor. The Court may, in the interest of public safety, order
the owner to have the vicious dog destroyed or may direct the appropriate
authorities to destroy the dog.
(Ord. 2006-06, passed 8-7-2006) Penalty, see §
90.99
§ 90.42 STRAY ANIMALS.
   (A)   This section shall also be entitled “The Boundary County Stray Animal
Ordinance.” It may be cited in Court as such.
   (B)   For the purposes of this section, the word ANIMAL shall include and
mean all beings commonly referred to and thought of as animals, except human
beings and those animals that are wild animals living in a wild state or
condition. However, this section shall only refer to and apply to those such
animals not covered and dealt with by the open range and herd district laws of
the state contained in the Idaho Code.
   (C)   It is hereby declared that any animal which is injurious to health, or
is indecent, or offensive to the senses, or an obstruction to the free use of
property, so as to interfere with the comfortable enjoyment of life or property
by an entire community or neighborhood, or by any considerable number of
persons, or unlawfully obstructs the free passage or use, in the customary
manner, of any navigable lake or river, stream, canal, or basin, or any public
park, square, street, or highway whether it be a county one or a state one, is
a public nuisance and is subject to the remedies found in the Idaho Code for
the abatement and punishment of all public nuisances, and also subject to the
enforcement provisions of Idaho Code §§ 52-201 et seq.
(Ord. 82-3, passed 12-13-1982) Penalty, see §
90.99
§ 90.99 PENALTY.
   (A)   Any person violating any provision of this chapter for which no
specific penalty is prescribed shall be subject to §
10.99 of this code of ordinances.
   (B)   Any person who violates any provision of §§
90.15 through
90.21 or §§
90.35 through
90.41 is guilty of a misdemeanor punishable as allowed by Idaho Code §§ 18-113
and 25-35 et seq. In addition to the misdemeanor penalty provision identified,
said person may be subject to civil remedies available at law and the owner’s
dog as identified above may be destroyed upon court order.
   (C)   (1)   Violation of §
90.42 is a misdemeanor and the punishment for a violation of §
90.42 can be either a fine in an amount not to exceed $300 or by confinement in
the county jail for a term not to exceed six months, or by both such fine and
imprisonment.
      (2)   For clarification purposes, be it known that each separate time
that an animal or animals are allowed to constitute a public nuisance in
violation of §
90.42 shall constitute a separate offense and that TIME means each separate
occurrence whereby the animal or animals constitute a public nuisance.
(Ord. 82-3, passed 12-13-1982; Ord. 2006-06, passed 8-7-2006)
CHAPTER 91: NOXIOUS WEEDS
Section
   
91.01   Purpose
   
91.02   Definitions
   
91.03   County enforcement duties
   
91.04   County authority
   
91.05   Notices for control of county noxious weeds
   
91.06   Landowner duties and rights
   
91.07   Weed Control Advisory Committees
   
91.08   Designation of county noxious weeds and action plan
   
91.09   Prevention of entry and dissemination of unknown plants and new alien
species
   
91.10   Exhibits
 
   
91.99   Penalty
§ 91.01 PURPOSE.
   The purpose of this chapter is to designate county noxious weeds, legal
requirements, duties, and responsibilities of parties to which the chapter
applies, and to provide the statutory and financial means for the control of
county noxious weeds.
(Ord. 94-1, passed 2-2-1994)
§ 91.02 DEFINITIONS.
   For the purpose of this chapter, the following definitions apply unless the
context clearly indicates or requires a different meaning.
   AGENCY.
      (1)   In the case of the federal government, any authority which
exercises administrative control over defined areas of federal lands;
      (2)   In the case of the state, any department, board, commission, or
institution; and
      (3)   In the case of local government, cities, counties, and any legal
subdivisions thereof, drainage districts, irrigation districts, canal
companies, highway districts, or any special taxing district.
   APPLICABLE FUND OR ACCOUNT. The county’s Noxious Weed Fund, which is hereby
created and established and shall be maintained in each county and which shall
be used exclusively for the purpose of fulfilling the county’s role in noxious
weed control.
   CONTAINMENT. Halting the spread of a county noxious weed infestation beyond
specified boundaries.
   COUNTY. Boundary County or its Board of County Commissioners or its Board of
County Commissioners’ authorized designee.
   COUNTY NOXIOUS WEED. Any plant which has been designated noxious by the
Board of County Commissioners in accordance with the county noxious weed review
and decision record shown in Exhibit 1 of this chapter.
   ERADICATION. The complete elimination of all above-ground plant growth of a
target county noxious weed species for a period of two years.
   LAND. All soil or water or other medium that supports plant growth.
   LANDOWNER.
      (1)   The person who holds legal title to the land, except that portion
for which another person has the right to use, exclude others from possession
of, or manage the parcel; or
      (2)   A person with an interest in a parcel of land such that the person
has the right to exclude others from possession of, use, or manage the parcel.
   PERSON. Any individual, partnership, firm, agency, corporation, company,
society, or association.
   PREVENTION. Any action that reduces the potential for the introduction or
establishment of a plant species in areas not currently infested with that
species.
   QUARANTINE. The regulation of the production, movement, or existence of
plants, plant products, animals, animal products, or any other article or
material, or the normal activity of persons, to prevent or limit introduction
or spread of county noxious weeds.
   RESTORATION. The process of reconditioning formerly weed-infested land to a
productive or desirable condition.
   SPECIAL MANAGEMENT ZONE. An area wherein specific requirements of this
chapter may be modified.
   WEED CONTROL. Any or all of the following: prevention, restoration,
eradication, or modified treatments specified in a special management zone.
(Ord. 94-1, passed 2-2-1994)
§ 91.03 COUNTY ENFORCEMENT DUTIES.
   (A)   The duty of enforcing this chapter and carrying out its provisions is
vested in the Board of County Commissioners.
   (B)   The Board of County Commissioners shall:
      (1)   Carry out the duties and responsibilities vested in the county
under this chapter and rules and regulations prescribed by the Board of County
Commissioners;
      (2)   Establish and maintain a coordinated program for control of county
noxious weeds in the county, and employ a County Weed Superintendent who shall
be qualified to detect and treat county noxious weeds;
      (3)   Designate one of its members as the liaison between the County Weed
Superintendent and the Board of County Commissioners;
      (4)   Provide operational and educational funds for the County Weed
Superintendent;
      (5)   Publish, between March 1 and April 30, in a newspaper of general
circulation within the county, a general notice for control of county noxious
weeds. The notice shall contain the list of county noxious weeds and identify
those known to be in the county, and shall stipulate the obligation to control.
Failure to publish the notice for control or serve individual notices herein
provided does not relieve any person from full compliance with this chapter and
regulations thereunder. Such publication shall be deemed legal and sufficient
notice;
      (6)   Cause, whenever the county finds it necessary to secure more prompt
or definite control of county noxious weeds than is accomplished by the general
notice, individual notices on a form approved by the Board of County
Commissioners to be served upon the landowner and, where possible, on the
operator of the land giving specific instructions when and how certain named
county noxious weeds are to be controlled. The individual notice shall also
contain information concerning the right to appeal. Individual notices shall be
applicable only to the current growing season;
      (7)   Ensure that all money received under the provisions of this chapter
shall be deposited to the county’s Noxious Weed Fund and shall be accounted for
as prescribed by the County Auditor, and that disbursements from the Noxious
Weed Fund shall be made only for noxious weed control purposes;
      (8)   Authorize and charge the County Weed Superintendent to:
         (a)   Examine all land within the county for the purpose of
determining whether the provisions of this chapter have been complied with;
         (b)   Maintain records, compile data, and submit such reports as the
Board of County Commissioners may require;
         (c)   Consult, advise, confer, and provide direction on matters
pertaining to county noxious weeds and the most effective and most practical
methods of county noxious weed control;
         (d)   Investigate or aid in the investigation and prosecution of any
violation of the provisions of this chapter, including serving of notices;
         (e)   Meet certification requirements as prescribed by the Board of
County Commissioners; and
         (f)   Make recommendations regarding establishment of special
management zones.
      (9)   Determine what weeds are noxious for the purposes of this chapter;
      (10)   Compile and keep current a list of such noxious weeds accurately
identified by both common and scientific name, which list shall be published
and incorporated in the rules and regulations of the Board of County
Commissioners in the format shown in Exhibit 2 of the ordinance codified
herein;
      (11)   Make and publish such rules and regulations as in the Board of
County Commissioners’ judgment are necessary to carry out the provisions of
this chapter; and
      (12)   Publish a list of things capable of carrying noxious weeds or
their seeds, and designate treatment of such articles to prevent their movement
of weeds.
(Ord. 94-1, passed 2-2-1994)
§ 91.04 COUNTY AUTHORITY.
   The Board of County Commissioners are authorized, but not limited, to:
   (A)   Investigate the subject of county noxious weeds;
   (B)   Cooperate and enter into agreements with persons and with federal,
state, and county agencies in carrying out the Board of County Commissioners’
duties under this chapter, and to conduct matters outside the county in the
interest of county noxious weed control;
   (C)   Enter, if there is probable cause for entry pursuant to this chapter,
and at reasonable times, upon all lands within the county where there are
noxious weeds, to examine the condition of such weeds, if a reasonable attempt
has been made to contact the landowner, and the manager of the land, prior to
entry;
   (D)   Call and attend meetings and conferences dealing with the subject of
county noxious weeds;
   (E)   Disseminate information and conduct informational campaigns;
   (F)   Inspect and certify the county’s crops, imports, and exports to verify
freedom from county noxious weeds, and authorize others to conduct such
inspections and certification;
   (G)   Apply to any court of competent jurisdiction for a search warrant
authorizing access to any land where access was sought for the purposes set
forth in this chapter but denied by the landowner; in which situation, the
court may, upon such application, issue a search warrant for purposes of this
chapter;
   (H)   Perform such other acts as may be necessary or appropriate to the
administration of the provisions of this chapter;
   (I)   Accept any funds or grants in aid from the state or federal government
for county noxious weed control purposes and account for such moneys as
prescribed by the State Auditor, and all such federal funds are hereby
appropriated to the purpose for which they are received;
   (J)   Permit modification of specific county noxious weed control
requirements in certain areas, after consulting with the landowner and
designating the area as a special management zone;
   (K)   Temporarily designate a plant species to be a county noxious weed for
up to 15 months after consultation with affected persons and publication in a
newspaper of general circulation serving the area of infestation;
   (L)   Have county noxious weeds controlled without cost to the landowner if
that is to the benefit of the county, without prejudice toward provisions of
this chapter relating to landowner responsibility to pay for costs of control;
   (M)   Quarantine any tract of land under its jurisdiction when it appears
there is an infestation of county noxious weeds beyond the ability of the
landowner to control and put into immediate operation the required means for
the control or containment of such county noxious weeds including necessary
destruction of crops;
   (N)   Stop movement of county noxious weed infested items. Such items shall
not be moved from designated premises except in accordance with the written
permission of the Board of County Commissioners or its designee;
   (O)   Purchase or provide for equipment and materials for the control of
county noxious weeds, independently or in combination with other control
authorities, and use such equipment or materials upon any lands within the
county;
   (P)   Utilize any other methods or local options that may be available for
the purpose of funding a coordinated county noxious weed control program;
   (Q)   Use the county’s Noxious Weed Fund, which may be a revolving fund,
only for county noxious weed purposes. In addition to any appropriated funds
designated for the control of county noxious weeds, the Board of County
Commissioners shall have the power to receive and disburse funds from any
source as a continuing appropriation at any time for the purpose of controlling
county noxious weeds; and
   (R)   Propose and accept plans for county noxious weed control which may be
extended over a period of years by agreement with the landowner. The agreement
shall be a contract and the county shall have the power and duty to enforce the
terms of any such agreement.
(Ord. 94-1, passed 2-2-1994)
§ 91.05 NOTICES FOR CONTROL OF COUNTY NOXIOUS WEEDS.
   (A)   Notices for control of county noxious weeds shall consist of two
kinds:
      (1)   A general notice for control of county noxious weeds to be
published in a newspaper of general circulation within the county; and
      (2)   Individual notices in a form prescribed by the Board of County
Commissioners.
   (B)   Failure to publish the notice for control or serve individual notices
herein provided does not relieve any person from full compliance with this
chapter and regulations thereunder. In all cases, said published notice shall
be deemed legal and sufficient notice. The general notice for control shall be
published at least one time between March 1 and April 30 and shall contain a
list of the county noxious weeds and shall specifically indicate those county
noxious weeds known to be in or imminently threatening to the county, and shall
stipulate the obligation to control.
   (C)   Whenever the county finds it necessary to secure more prompt or
definite control of county noxious weeds than is accomplished by the general
notice, it shall cause individual notices to be served upon the person or
agency owning and controlling such land, giving specific instructions when and
how certain named weeds are to be controlled. The individual notice shall also
contain information concerning the right to appeal. Individual notices shall be
applicable only to the current growing season.
   (D)   (1)   Whenever the owner of any land on which county noxious weeds are
present has neglected or failed to initiate control as required pursuant to
this chapter within five working days from receipt of an individual notice
given pursuant to this section, the county having jurisdiction shall have
proper control methods used on such land, including necessary destruction of
crops, and shall advise the owner of the cost incurred in connection with such
operation. The cost of any such control on private land shall be at the expense
of the owner.
      (2)   If unpaid for 60 days or longer, the amount of such expense shall
become a lien upon the property. Thereafter, the lien shall be subject to
collection by the county by sale of the property in the same manner as for
delinquent taxes. If the costs have not been paid to the person that performed
the work within 60 days, the Board of County Commissioners may direct that suit
be brought in a court of competent jurisdiction for the unpaid charges. Nothing
contained in this section shall be construed to require satisfaction of the
imposed obligation by the sale of property or to bar the application of any
other available remedy. Amounts collected under this section shall be deposited
to the county’s Noxious Weed Fund.
   (E)   When it appears to the County Noxious Weed Superintendent that upon
any tract of land under county jurisdiction there is an infestation of county
noxious weeds beyond the ability of the owner of such land to control, the
county may quarantine such land and put into immediate operation the necessary
means for the control of such county noxious weeds including necessary
destruction of crops. The county shall, prior to the entry upon such land,
serve individual notice on the owner thereof of such quarantine and of the date
of the proposed entry, and shall also advise the same persons of the completion
of the control operation and the cost thereof. If the owner is not known or
readily available, notice shall be deemed satisfied after eight days from
postmark of registered mail to the address as shown on the assessment roll of
the county.
(Ord. 94-1, passed 2-2-1994)
§ 91.06 LANDOWNER DUTIES AND RIGHTS.
   (A)   Duties.
      (1)   It shall be the duty and responsibility of all landowners to
control county noxious weeds on his, her, or their land and property in
accordance with this chapter and with rules and regulations promulgated by the
Board of County Commissioners.
      (2)   The cost of controlling county noxious weeds shall be the
obligation of the landowner.
      (3)   County noxious weed control must be for prevention, eradication, or
restoration. However, areas may be modified from the eradication requirement
after the Board of County Commissioners have determined them to be unreasonable
for short-term eradication and have designated the area as a special management
zone.
      (4)   The landowner shall reimburse the county for work done by the
county because of the landowner’s failure to comply with this chapter.
      (5)   The landowner shall not move any article that is infested with
county noxious weeds from the landowner’s premises until it is treated in
accordance with the applicable regulations, or if quarantine applies, in
accordance with the written permission of the Board of County Commissioners or
its designee.
   (B)   Rights.
      (1)   Any landowner who is dissatisfied with the control measures used or
the manner in which control is conducted upon his or her property, or with the
amount of any charge by the county for control work or for the purchase of
materials or use of equipment, may, within 30 days after being advised of the
amount of the charge, file a protest with the Board of County Commissioners.
      (2)   Any person served with an individual notice may, within two days of
receipt of the notice, appeal to the Board of County Commissioners. A hearing
shall be set by the Board of County Commissioners within five days after
receipt of notice of the appeal. Notice of the hearing shall be sent by the
Board of County Commissioners to the appellant.
(Ord. 94-1, passed 2-2-1994)
§ 91.07 WEED CONTROL ADVISORY COMMITTEES.
   (A)   The Board of County Commissioners may appoint persons to a Weed
Control Advisory Committee, who shall be persons knowledgeable of county
noxious weeds and the damage done by such weeds. The members of the Advisory
Committee shall be appointed for renewable terms of two years.
   (B)   It shall be the function of each Weed Control Advisory Committee to:
      (1)   Assist in planning and carrying out county noxious weed control
programs;
      (2)   Act as liaison to other Weed Control Advisory Committees; and
      (3)   Provide a forum for public input on matters relating to the control
of county noxious weeds.
   (C)   Members of the Advisory Committee may be reimbursed for actual and
necessary expenses when on committee business. Expense payments may be made
from the county’s Noxious Weed Fund.
   (D)   Advisory Committees have no executive powers and act in an advisory
capacity only.
(Ord. 94-1, passed 2-2-1994)
§ 91.08 DESIGNATION OF COUNTY NOXIOUS WEEDS AND ACTION PLAN.
   (A)   Any county resident may submit to the Board of County Commissioners a
request for county noxious weed designation of a species if such request is in
writing and includes reasons against a proposed plan for county action on such
species. All non-native plant species reliably reported to exist in the county,
and all requests for county noxious weed designation, must be considered by the
Weed Control Advisory Committee for recommendation to the Board of County
Commissioners within six months of the request. Records of all such
considerations must be maintained on permanent file by the Board of County
Commissioners.
   (B)   (1)   Every species for which county noxious weed designation is
required or deemed feasible, including species considered for temporary
designation, shall, except as otherwise herein provided, be designated a county
noxious weed only upon recommendation from the County Weed Control Advisory
Committee and subsequent approval by the Board of County Commissioners, using
the format shown in Exhibit 1 of the ordinance codified herein.
      (2)    County noxious weeds under this chapter shall be so designated by
a separate resolution of the Board of County Commissioners and are subject to
change in designation upon review by the County Weed Control Advisory Committee
and subsequent action of the Board of County Commissioners, except as otherwise
herein provided. County noxious weeds shall be so designated only upon meeting
criteria provided in Exhibit 1 of the ordinance codified herein, and shall be
listed by both common and scientific names using the format shown in Exhibit 2
of the ordinance codified herein.
      (3)   Any species designated noxious pursuant to this chapter shall
automatically and immediately cease to be so designated if and when that
species is designated noxious under the Idaho Code.
(Ord. 94-1, passed 2-2-1994)
§ 91.09 PREVENTION OF ENTRY AND DISSEMINATION OF UNKNOWN PLANTS AND NEW ALIEN
SPECIES.
   Since serious weeds often become established because their noxious potential
is not recognized until they have spread beyond possibility of eradication,
protection of the county against unrecognized threats shall include the
following.
   (A)   Unknown plants. No plant, including seed or other propagules, of any
alien species shall be grown in, transported within, or admitted into the
county without accurate taxonomic identification as to the species by either
Latin name or by common name listed in locally available standard reference
books. All unknown plants are hereby designated county noxious weeds. If such
plants are subsequently identified in accordance with this chapter to the
satisfaction of the Board of County Commissioners, the noxious status of such
plants is determined by that identification.
   (B)   New alien species. Any plant species found in the county, and that has
not been documented to previously exist in the county, shall be automatically
and immediately designated a county noxious weed on a temporary basis. Such
species may be subsequently designated otherwise by the Board of County
Commissioners after further investigation and completion of the noxious weed
review and decision record in Exhibit 1 of the ordinance codified herein.
(Ord. 94-1, passed 2-2-1994)
§ 91.10 EXHIBITS.
   Exhibits 1 and 2 are included in the ordinance codified and are adopted
herein as if set out in full. They are to be used as guides for criteria,
planning, and listing. Actual formats may vary if the nature of individual
species or their infestations so warrant, in the judgment of the Weed Control
Advisory Committee.
(Ord. 94-1, passed 2-2-1994)
§ 91.99 PENALTY.
   (A)   Any person knowing of the existence of any county noxious weeds on
lands owned or controlled by him or her, who fails to control such weeds in
accordance with this chapter or any person who intrudes upon any land under
quarantine or who moves or causes to be moved any article covered by this
chapter except as provided or who prevents or threatens to prevent entry upon
land as provided in this chapter, or who interferes with the carrying out of
the provisions of this chapter or who violates any of the provisions of this
chapter, shall be guilty of a misdemeanor and shall be subject to a fine not to
exceed $1,000 or up to one year in jail, or both such fine and imprisonment for
each violation.
   (B)   The Board of County Commissioners may bring an action to enforce the
provisions of this chapter and the penalty provided for under this section.
(Ord. 94-1, passed 2-2-1994)

**** GENERAL PROVISIONS ****
§ 110.01 LIQUOR SALES.
   (A)   From the effective date of this section, in accordance with the
provisions of Idaho Code § 23-927(2), the hours when liquor may be sold in the
county are hereby extended to 2:00 a.m., prevailing local time.
   (B)   From the effective date of this section, in accordance with the
provisions of Idaho Code § 23-927(2), liquor may be sold in the county on
Sundays, Memorial Day, and Thanksgiving Day.
(Ord. 86-3, passed 7-7-1986)
**** LICENSES ****
§ 110.15 AUTHORITY.
   Pursuant to Idaho Code §§ 23-9 et seq. and 23-10 et seq. from and after the
passage hereof, no county application for issuance, renewal, or transfer shall
be granted for a retail beer, wine, or liquor license until approval thereof by
the Board of County Commissioners.
(Ord. 2000-05, passed 5-9-2000)
§ 110.16 FEES.
   A copy of the county application shall be submitted to the Clerk of the
Board of County Commissioners. The applicant shall tender the required fees for
the county license as adopted by resolution.
(Ord. 2000-05, passed 5-9-2000)
§ 110.17 ADMINISTRATION AND PROCEDURES.
   (A)   Upon submission of a completed application, the Board of County
Commissioners shall take action at its next available public meeting. Upon
completion of the proceedings by the Board of County Commissioners, if the
application is approved, a license shall be issued upon receipt of all required
documentation.
   (B)   If the application is denied, said notification shall specify:
      (1)   The statutes, ordinances, and standards used in evaluating the
application;
      (2)   The reasons for the denial; and
      (3)   The actions, if any, the applicant can take to obtain the license,
transfer, or renewal thereof.
(Ord. 2000-05, passed 5-9-2000)
§ 110.18 TRANSCRIBABLE RECORD.
   Proceedings before the Board of County Commissioners shall be recorded by a
transcribable verbatim recording of the proceedings which shall be maintained
for a period of six months, unless judicial review is sought as provided by
Idaho Code § 23-1015, in which case they shall be preserved until all judicial
remedy is exhausted. The applicant, upon written request and upon payment of
the estimated cost, may have the record transcribed within the periods
prescribed in this section. The county, in addition to the transcribable
record, shall maintain minutes of the meeting to be retained indefinitely, or
as otherwise provided by law.
(Ord. 2000-05, passed 5-9-2000)

**** GENERAL PROVISIONS ****
§ 111.01 INTENT.
   It is necessary for the protection of the health, safety, and welfare of the
general public to license and regulate the advertising, permitting, or
conducting of certain educational, religious, entertainment, or amusement
activities which will or can be reasonably expected to disturb the ordinary
peace and quiet of the inhabitants of residences, dwelling houses, or other
ordinary places of human habitation.
(Ord. 1972-1, passed 1-24-1972)
§ 111.02 PERMIT REQUIRED.
   It shall be unlawful for any person, persons, corporation, landowner, or
lessor to allow, conduct, permit, advertise, or cause to be conducted,
permitted, or advertised, any entertainment, religious, educational, or
amusement activity or activities outside the municipal boundaries of the
incorporated cities with the county, and which said activity or activities
will, or can reasonably be expected to attract an outdoor gathering or assembly
of 1,000 or more persons at any one time (whether or not a charge or
contribution is required for admission), unless a valid county permit has first
been obtained, authorizing and permitting the conduct of such activity or
activities; provided, however, that no such permit shall be required for any
such activity or activities authorized under any valid lease of real property
owned by the county, the state, or any political subdivision thereof.
(Ord. 1972-1, passed 1-24-1972) Penalty, see §
111.99
§ 111.03 APPLICATION FOR PERMIT.
   Written application for any permit to conduct the activities regulated by
this chapter shall be made to the Board of County Commissioners not less than
60 days prior to the date upon which such activities are to commence. Said
application shall include:
   (A)   The name of the person, persons, partnership, corporation, or
organization on whose behalf such application is made;
   (B)   The names and addresses of all persons having any proprietary interest
in any such partnership, corporation, or organization;
   (C)   The principal place of business of any such partnership, corporation,
or organization;
   (D)   The legal description of the land to be occupied or utilized by such
activities, together with the address of the owner or lessee thereof and a
verified consent by said owner (if other than the applicant) permitting the
activity or activities upon said real property upon the dates for which the
application is made;
   (E)   A description of the type and nature of the educational, religious,
entertainment, or amusement activity or activities for which a permit is
sought;
   (F)   The date or dates upon which such activity or activities are proposed
to be conducted; and
   (G)   The maximum number of people expected to attend, view, and participate
in said activity or activities on each date applied for. Written notice of
approval or disapproval of said application shall be given to the applicant no
later than 20 days after the application has been filed. Any notice of denial
shall state the regulations, laws, and/or conditions upon which denial is based
and the nature of the noncompliance by the applicant. Within ten days after
mailing of such notice, the applicant may show that such noncompliance has been
remedied; upon failure of the applicant to do so, the denial shall be final and
conclusive.
(Ord. 1972-1, passed 1-24-1972)
§ 111.04 APPLICATION FEE.
   The basic permit fee which shall accompany any application hereunder shall
be $100, which said fee shall be retained by the county whether or not a permit
be granted, denied, or withdrawn.
(Ord. 1972-1, passed 1-24-1972)
§ 111.05 SUBMISSION OF PLANS FOR APPROVAL; APPROVING AGENCIES.
   Whenever approval by any governmental agency other than the county is
required hereunder, the applicant for such approval shall be required to
cooperate fully with such agency to ensure that a full review of the proposals
may be accomplished by such agency within the 20-day time limit set out in §
111.03. When any type of physical facility is required or subject to approval
hereunder, a permit may be granted (subject to withdrawal) based upon specific
plans being proposed, submitted, and approved hereunder. All such facilities
constructed under authority of such permit shall be in existence at least five
days prior to the commencement of the activity or activities for which the
permit is granted and shall be subject to inspection by the approving agency or
agencies. Should the actual facility or construction fail to meet the standards
approved in the proposed plans, the permit may be withdrawn. Withdrawal shall
be effected by mailing of written notice to the address of the applicant
specifying the reasons for such withdrawal.
(Ord. 1972-1, passed 1-24-1972)
§ 111.06 FAILURE TO COMPLY SHALL CONSTITUTE A PUBLIC NUISANCE.
   Noncompliance with the minimum health, sanitation, and safety provisions
required herein, or failure to comply with the terms and conditions hereunder
shall constitute a public nuisance, and the person or persons responsible
therefor shall be subject to all criminal and civil remedies for such.
(Ord. 1972-1, passed 1-24-1972)
**** CONDITIONS ****
§ 111.20 CONDITIONS FOR ISSUANCE OF PERMITS.
   Permits shall be issued hereunder only when the conditions enumerated
hereinafter in §§
111.21 through
111.28 have been fulfilled by the applicant.
(Ord. 1972-1, passed 1-24-1972)
§ 111.21 HEALTH AND SANITARY FACILITIES.
   No permit shall be granted hereunder unless the applicants obtain the
written approval of a medical doctor duly licensed in the state and the County
Health Officer and approved by the Board of County Commissioners, which said
health approvals shall indicate that the applicants for said permit have
complied with all rules, regulations, laws, and ordinances of the State Board
of Health, the Panhandle District Board of Health, the state, and the
Environmental Health Code. Such approval shall indicate the type and adequacy
of water supply to be provided, the type and adequacy of toilet, waste
collection, and washing facilities to be provided, the type and adequacy of
health and medical facilities to be provided, and, if there is to be food or
drink served on the premises, the type and adequacy of food and drink service
and facilities to be furnished.
(Ord. 1972-1, passed 1-24-1972)
§ 111.22 FIRE PROTECTION.
   No permit shall be granted hereunder unless the Board has received the
written approval of the Fire Protection District in which the proposed activity
or activities are to be held. Such approval shall indicate that the applicant
has complied with all applicable fire prevention regulations, laws, and
requirements and shall state the manner in which the applicant has complied
therewith.
(Ord. 1972-1, passed 1-24-1972)
§ 111.23 DISTANCE FROM HABITATION.
   No permit shall be granted for any activity or activities regulated
hereunder which are proposed to be held within 1,000 yards of any schoolhouse
or church; or within 500 yards of any occupied house, residence, or other place
of human habitation; provided, however, that such restriction with respect to
occupied houses, residences, and other places of human habitation may be waived
upon proof by the applicant that the occupants of such places have given his,
her, or their written consent to the conduct of such activity or activities.
(Ord. 1972-1, passed 1-24-1972)
§ 111.24 PARKING FACILITIES.
   No permit shall be granted for any activity or activities regulated
hereunder unless the applicant shows to the Board of County Commissioners, by
means of a scale drawing, that adequate parking facilities have been made
available within or adjacent to the location for which the permit is requested.
Such parking facilities shall provide parking space for one vehicle for each
four persons expected or reasonably expected to attend, view, or participate in
the proposed activity or activities. Such parking facilities shall be provided
off public roads or highways and adequate ingress and egress shall be provided
to and from such parking areas.
(Ord. 1972-1, passed 1-24-1972)
§ 111.25 HOURS OF OPERATION.
   No permit issued hereunder shall authorize any activity or activities
regulated hereby between the hours of 12:01 a.m. and 9:00 a.m. of any day; said
permit shall specify on its face the day, or days, and hours within which such
activity or activities may be held.
(Ord. 1972-1, passed 1-24-1972)
§ 111.26 PUBLIC SAFETY.
   No permit shall be granted hereunder unless the applicant has obtained the
written approval of the Sheriff, indicating that the following conditions have
been complied with:
   (A)   Adequate traffic control and crowd protection policing have been
contracted for or otherwise provided by the applicant;
   (B)   Traffic and control personnel shall be named persons deputized by the
Sheriff, or meeting the criteria for becoming so deputized;
   (C)   The applicant will provide at least one traffic control officer for
each 500 persons expected or reasonably expected to be in attendance at any one
time during the event; and
   (D)   The applicant will provide at least one crowd control officer for each
250 persons expected or reasonably expected to be in attendance at any time
during the event; provided, however, that if at any time during the said event
the size of the crowd exceeds by 20% the number of persons represented by the
applicant as expected to be in attendance, the Sheriff shall have the
discretion to require the limitation of further admissions.
(Ord. 1972-1, passed 1-24-1972)
§ 111.27 CASH BOND AND INDEMNIFICATIONS.
   No permit shall be issued hereunder unless the applicant has on deposit with
the County Treasurer a cash or surety bond as set out hereinbelow indemnifying
the county or any political subdivision within said county for costs incurred
by reason of the expenses of extraordinary law enforcement, damage to public
property, or the costs of providing any extraordinary sanitation or health
services provided or caused by the holding of the activity or activities for
which the permit is applied for. The amount of such bond shall be $5,000 for
gatherings where the total attendance is expected to be less than 5,000 persons
and such amount shall be increased in increments of $2,500 for each additional
2,500 persons expected. The bond or its balance shall be exonerated or returned
when the Board of County Commissioners certify to the County Treasurer that
either:
   (A)   No damage has been done; or
   (B)   The total of such costs or damage in the event such items total less
than the bond amount.
(Ord. 1972-1, passed 1-24-1972)
§ 111.28 LIABILITY INSURANCE.
   No permit shall be issued hereunder unless the applicant has furnished
evidence of liability insurance coverage providing a minimum of $100,000 bodily
injury coverage per person, $300,000 bodily injury coverage per occurrence, and
$100,000 property damage coverage, which policy shall name the county as an
additional insured.
(Ord. 1972-1, passed 1-24-1972)
§ 111.99 PENALTY.
   Any person who shall violate or fail to comply with any of the provisions of
this chapter, or who, having obtained a permit hereunder, shall willfully fail
to continue to comply with the conditions herein set forth, shall be deemed
guilty of a misdemeanor, and upon conviction thereof, shall be punished by a
fine not to exceed $300 or by imprisonment in the county jail for a period not
exceeding six months, or by both such fine and imprisonment.
(Ord. 1972-1, passed 1-24-1972)

§ 130.01 GAS-POWERED ENGINES ON ROBINSON LAKE NOT TO EXCEED 15 HORSEPOWER.
   It shall be lawful to operate any watercraft or boat on Robinson Lake
powered by an internal combustion engine so long as the internal combustion
engine produces no more than 15 horsepower.
(Ord. 2025-3, passed 12-10-2024) Penalty, see §
130.99
§ 130.02 DISPOSING OF CERTAIN ITEMS IN THE COUNTY LANDFILL PROHIBITED.
   (A)   Purpose. This section shall be deemed an exercise of the police power
of the county acting in its governmental capacity for the protection and
preservation of the health, welfare, safety, and cleanliness of the public and
public areas, and all the provisions shall be liberally construed for the
accomplishment of that purpose.
   (B)   Restrictions.
      (1)   It shall be unlawful for any person to dispose of, or attempt to
dispose of, in any manner, the following at any place other than the county
landfill: furniture, bedsprings, mattresses, bath tubs, toilets, household
appliances occupying over two cubic feet in space, waste from the construction
or deconstruction of any structure or building including, but not limited to,
lumber of any kind, concrete, rock, sand and gravel, heavy and bulk logging
waste, tree limbs, stumps and brush, heavy and bulk farm waste, vegetable and/
or fruit over 100 pounds, fence posts of any kind, wire of any kind, tires,
batteries or battery acids, combustible engines or engine blocks, motor oils,
solvents of any kind, bulk liquids in excess of five gallons, animal carcasses,
processed animal or human excrement except that properly contained within
disposable diapers, any hazardous materials other than household waste. None of
these items shall be disposed of in the county dump boxes.
      (2)   The county landfill and rural monitor sites will take used motor
oil, used antifreeze, glass, newspapers, magazines, cardboard, tin and aluminum
cans, which can be recycled at no cost.
      (3)   Manned rural sites will take wood including tree trimmings, shrubs/
brush, clean untreated/unpainted lumber, metal including wire, household
appliances under two cubic feet, sheet metal, and rims.
      (4)   Any load of wood or metal over two cubic yards must be disposed of
at the county landfill.
      (5)   All persons utilizing the county landfill or any manned or unmanned
collection sites must obey all notices and signs and the directions of site
personnel associated with the disposal of items in the county landfill or any
manned or unmanned rural site.
      (6)   These restrictions may be amended and modified by rules and
regulations adopted by resolution concerning the operation of the county
landfill and any manned or unmanned rural collection site.
(Ord. 96-04, passed 3-19-1996; Ord. 2008-01, passed 11-19-2007) Penalty, see §
130.99
Cross-reference:
   Solid wastes, see
Ch. 50
§ 130.99 PENALTY.
   (A)   Any person violating any provision of this chapter for which no
specific penalty is prescribed shall be subject to §
10.99 of this code of ordinances.
   (B)   The operation of any watercraft or boat on Robinson Lake powered by an
internal combustion engine producing greater than 15 horsepower as stated in §
130.01 shall be unlawful and shall constitute a misdemeanor offense punishable
by imprisonment in the county jail for a period not exceeding six months, a
fine not to exceed $500, or both.
   (C)   Any person who violates the provisions of §

of any of the provisions in §
130.02 shall be deemed a separate offense.

CHAPTER 131: OFFENSES AGAINST REAL PROPERTY
Section
   
131.01   Real property prohibited uses
   
131.02   Snowmobile Park prohibited uses
 
   
131.99   Penalty
§ 131.01 REAL PROPERTY PROHIBITED USES.
   (A)   Purpose. The purpose of this section is to regulate and provide
penalties for violations of certain uses of county real property.
   (B)   Short title. This section shall be known and may be cited as “Boundary
County Real Properly Prohibited Uses.”
   (C)   Owner of real property. The county is the owner of real property
located in county, as follows: the County Fairgrounds and Park, Deep Creek Boat
Launch, Rocky Point Boat Launch, Copeland Boat Launch, Porthill Boat Launch,
and Riverside Park.
   (D)   Prohibited uses.
      (1)   It shall be unlawful for any person or persons to discharge any
firearm on the properties described in division (C) above belonging to the
county. A FIREARM shall include, but not be limited to, a BB gun, pellet guns,
and any caliber of rifle, handgun, shotgun, semi-automatic, or automatic
firearm.
      (2)   It shall be unlawful for any person or persons to camp on the
properties described in division (C) above belonging to the county for more
than three consecutive nights. Nights spent at different locations shall be
considered CONSECUTIVE for purposes of this section, and campers may not evade
this limitation by moving between different campsites owned by the county. The
county reserves the right to alter these requirements at particular locations
as circumstances dictate. Such changes may be accomplished by resolution of the
Board of County Commissioners. If a different requirement is adopted by the
Board of County Commissioners, such requirement shall be in full force and
effect only upon the posting of clear signage at the site in question which
provides the public with notice of the requirements at that location.
      (3)   It shall be unlawful for any person or persons who own or are
caretaking an animal to leave the animal unattended on the properties described
in division (C) above belonging to the county. All animals shall be required to
be on a leash. The owner or caretaker of the animal shall immediately clean up
any animal defecation and dispose of it in a trash can.
      (4)   It shall be unlawful for any person or persons to cause any loud or
obnoxious noises from 8:00 p.m. to 8:00 a.m. every day on the properties
described in division (C) above belonging to the county. It shall be unlawful
to run a generator for any purpose from 8:00 p.m. to 8:00 a.m. every day on the
properties described in division (C) above belonging to the county.
      (5)   Any approved activity of the county and/or the County Parks and
Recreation and/or County Fair Board, shall be excluded from provisions of this
section.
(Ord. 2025-2, passed 12-10-2024) Penalty, see §
131.99
§ 131.02 SNOWMOBILE PARK PROHIBITED USES.
   (A)   Purpose. The purpose of this section is to regulate and provide
penalties for violations of certain uses of the County Snowmobile Park located
in the county.
   (B)   Short title. This section shall be known and may be cited as the
“County Snowmobile Park Prohibited Uses.”
   (C)   Owner of real property. The county is the owner of real property
(commonly referred to as the Snowmobile Park) located in the county more
particularly described as follows: Commencing at the southwest corner of
section 34, township 61 north, range one west, thence north along the west line
of said section 34 a distance of 484 feet to a point, thence east 450 feet to a
point, thence south 484 feet to the south section line, thence west along south
line a distance of 450 feet to a point of beginning containing approximately
five acres.
   (D)   Prohibited uses.
      (1)   It shall be unlawful for any person or persons to discharge any
firearm. A FIREARM shall include, but not be limited to, a BB gun, pellet guns,
and any caliber of rifle, handgun, shotgun, semi-automatic, or automatic
firearm.
      (2)   It shall be unlawful for any person or persons to camp on the
property for more than three consecutive nights.
      (3)   It shall be unlawful for any person or persons who own or are
caretaking an animal to leave the animal unattended. All animals shall be
required to be on a leash. The owner or caretaker of the animal shall
immediately clean up any animal defecation and dispose of it in a trash can.
      (4)   It shall be unlawful for any person or persons to cause any loud or
obnoxious noises from 8:00 p.m. to 8:00 a.m. everyday. It shall be unlawful to
run a generator for any purpose from 8:00 p.m. to 8:00 a.m. everyday.
(Ord. 2014-1, passed 8-25-2014) Penalty, see §
131.99
§ 131.99 PENALTY.
   (A)   Any person violating any provision of this chapter for which no
specific penalty is prescribed shall be subject to §
10.99 of this code of ordinances.
   (B)   The violation of any provision of §
131.01 shall be deemed a misdemeanor punishable by a fine not to exceed $300,
imprisonment in the county jail for a period not to exceed six months, or both
fine and imprisonment.
   (C)   The violation of any provision of §
131.02 shall be deemed a misdemeanor punishable by a fine not to exceed $300,
imprisonment in the county jail for a period not to exceed six months, or both
fine and imprisonment.
(Ord. 2014-1, passed 8-25-2014; Ord. 2025-2, passed 12-10-2024)
